One `Y.Text`; concurrent edits to different clusters merge, same-region edits surface as conflict hunks (`op-log-merge-conflict`); semantic label (`move`/`merge`/`split`/`rename`/`set-policy`) rides op `metadata` |
| `trees-module-discipline` | planned | All outline parse/serialize + metadata-frontmatter (de)serialization stays behind the `core::trees` boundary; outside callers consume `TreeRow` / `EditableNode` / `NodeKind` / `NodePolicy` — plain serde Rust in `core/src/trees/types.rs`. Mirrors `core::trails` (markdown, no on-disk-shape leakage). No SQLite, no schema-version file, no migration code. Submodule layout: `mod.rs`, `types.rs` (DTOs + `Trees` handle), `store.rs` (outline parse/serialize + op-log writes), `ops/{edit,move_node,merge,drop,folder_rename,split,rollup}.rs` |
| `tree-md-outline-body` | planned | The tree's structure is the editable outline in the `.md` body (not frontmatter): headings = clusters (depth = heading level), nested bullets = deeper clusters + leaves, leaf = a bullet wikilink, summary = the paragraph under a heading. Frontmatter holds tree metadata only |
| `tree-md-outline-grammar` | planned | Outline grammar: `#`…`######` heading or bold bullet = cluster; its text = name; the paragraph under it = summary; `- [[path]]` = leaf; inline `{move:…}`/`{tag:…}`/`{freeze}` (+`review`) on the heading = policy (`tree-policy-inline`); `Outliers` = conventionally-named bucket heading |
| `tree-md-parse-tiers` | planned | Lenient body parse: resolvable wikilink bullet → leaf; plain-text bullet → freeform text leaf (cf. `board-freeform-card`); unresolvable wikilink → broken leaf (`tree-broken-leaf`); malformed line (orphan heading, half-written `[[`) → skipped + decorated as an editor parse error, never dropped. Interleaved prose + unknown frontmatter preserved on round-trip |
| `tree-leaf-path-ref` | planned | A leaf references a note by vault-relative path (the wikilink target), no stamped id — same path-as-identity model as `board-card-references`. The leaf is the only pointer to the note |
| `tree-broken-leaf` | planned | A leaf whose path doesn't resolve renders greyed with a broken-reference pill and stays in its cluster for the user to repoint or remove — same safety net as a broken board card |
| `tree-leaf-rename-rewrite` | planned | `core::trees::on_note_moved` rewrites affected leaf paths in the same transaction as a note move, riding the shared `wikilink-rename-rewrite` pass alongside boards / trails / wikilink bodies |
| `tree-leaves-derived-table` | planned | Derived `tree_leaves` table in `index.db` for the affected-trees reverse lookup (`trees_containing_note`, symmetric to `boards_containing_note`); re-derived on tree-doc ingest, fail-loud on schema bump — mirrors `board-cards-derived-table` |
| `tree-cluster-path-identity` | planned | Clusters have no persisted id; identity is position-and-name in the outline (conceptual folders, nothing moves on disk). Node ids are ephemeral parse-time handles. Centroids keyed by cluster path, recomputed on membership change. Enables several overlapping trees over one vault |
| `tree-policy-inline` | planned | A cluster's policy is an inline attribute on its heading (`{move: <folder>}` / `{tag: <slug>}` / `{freeze}`, `review` for require-review). Authored content — survives round-trip and travels on copy-paste of a cluster |
| `tree-edit-via-tabs` | planned | Two editing surfaces over one op-log doc: the tree `.md` as an ordinary editor tab (inherits find / wikilink-hover / fonts / parse-error decorations) and the graphical tree/graph tab. Both-at-once via existing split-view + `tabs-linked-targeting`; the graphical view re-derives leniently from the doc. No bespoke dual-editor or sync layer |
| `tree-author-blank` | planned | "New tree → Empty" creates a metadata-only tree doc with an empty body; the user authors clusters as headings and adds notes as wikilinks (typed, or via `note-multi-select` drop). A hand-built two-cluster policy tree is the canonical by-example triage classifier; centroids derive from hand-assigned members |
| `tree-multi-placement` | planned | Default strict partition (a note appears once). The user may manually place the same wikilink under a second cluster. Conflict rules: Tag policies union; conflicting Move takes the highest-confidence cluster, else routes to review. Automatic soft/overlapping placement is deferred to the GMM work |
| `cluster-editor-draft-persistence` | planned | every edit (graphical or hand-typed) writes the tree `.md` through the op-log granular user-save path; the body outline is the structure (no parallel store); the draft survives app restarts via the persisted `.md` |
| `cluster-editor-edit-history` | planned | edits are op-log ops on the tree doc — no `cluster_tree_history` table; author/timestamp ride the op-log side table, the semantic op name rides op `metadata`. The build pass writes the initial outline |
| `cluster-editor-undo-redo` | planned | in-memory session undo/redo stack; each step applies the reverse edit through the working layer. Cross-session "revert to an earlier state" rides the tree doc's version history / snapshots, not a bespoke per-tree log |
| `cluster-editor-discard-draft` | planned | `source = one-shot` → delete the tree's `.md` via `core::ops::delete` (lands in trash, restorable like any note); saved-triage → "Unsave as triage" flips `state` back to `draft` and optionally deletes (user chooses) |
| `cluster-editor-markdown-view-toggle` | planned | editable markdown view over the outline body as a third view variant alongside Tree / Graph, mirroring `board-view-toggle`. Editing the markdown edits the tree (parses back via `tree-md-parse-tiers`); switching to graphical re-renders from the body. The same doc also opens as a full editor tab (`tree-edit-via-tabs`) |
| `cluster-editor-apply-action` | done | `core/src/suggest.rs::apply_tree` walks the tree, runs `resolve_effective_policy` per leaf, and emits one `staging.db` row per `Tag` / `Move` leaf — `surface = "cluster-editor"`, `metadata.tree_id`, `metadata.matched_node_id`, `metadata.policy_kind`, `metadata.require_review`, `metadata.tree_member_fingerprint`. Move rows carry `source_path`; Tag rows carry the pre-merged content + `source_hash` for drift safety. Skipped leaves (frozen / unpolicied / missing-note) are counted onto `ApplyOutcome`. `cluster_apply` command + UI `cluster-editor-pane-mode` Apply button + auto-flip of tree `state` → `applied` once every emitted row resolves |
| `cluster-editor-save-as-triage` | partial | `app/src/panels/cluster_review/mod.rs` enables the Save-as-triage button when the tree's `state != "saved-as-triage"`. Clicking confirms then calls `cluster_tree_set_state(tree_id, "saved-as-triage")` (Sprint A's `Trees::set_tree_state` free-string setter). Subsequent on-save events fire the classifier against the saved tree. Replaces any prior saved tree implicitly — the spec's one-saved-tree-per-vault rule is enforced by the `state == "saved-as-triage"` predicate. **Partial**: functional only via the batch-review pane button (`cluster_review/mod.rs:28`); the sidebar entry-point shortcut isn't ported (`app/src/clusters/sidebar/mod.rs:9` — "the pane button covers it") |
| `cluster-editor-regenerate-via-task-queue` | done | `cluster_regenerate_names` host command walks every cluster node, skips fully user-edited ones, and submits one `TaskKind::RaptorSummarize { tree_id, cluster_node_id, level: 0 }` task at `Priority::Normal`. Toolbar "Regenerate names" button + the per-row `↻ N` staleness badge both invoke it. Worker that consumes the task (resolves members → LLM call → writes back through `Trees::set_summary`) rides the existing direct-LLM drain. **Ordering:** tasks are submitted bottom-up — nodes grouped by depth from root (`BTreeMap<usize, Vec<EditableNode>>`), each depth's batch is `await_outcome`'d before the next shallower batch is enqueued, so parent summaries see their children's freshly-generated names instead of placeholders ("Cluster 1", "Cluster 2") |
| `cluster-editor-llm-actions-via-task-queue` | partial | The producer side now routes through `core::tasks`: regenerate (`cluster_regenerate_names`), staleness-badge regenerate, triage classifier dispatch (`cluster_triage_enqueue`). **Partial:** the auto-name-on-merge / auto-name-on-split fan-out is still inline-deterministic naming; promoting those to task submissions is a follow-up tied to the LLM-backed `Summarizer` impl |
| `cluster-editor-triage-on-save` | done | The watcher-subscriber spawn in the host (after `spawn_staging_recheck`) consumes every `FileEvent::Modified` / `Created`, applies the `[suggestions.triage].scope` prefix pre-filter, resolves the note's id + embedding from `read_store`, and runs `core::suggest::triage_all_saved_trees` synchronously (microseconds; no LLM). The async/queued variant is `cluster_triage_enqueue` per `cluster-editor-triage-via-task-queue` |
| `cluster-editor-triage-scheduled-rerun` | partial | the host — `parse_rerun_interval` parses a duration-string grammar (`30m`, `1h`, `6h`, `24h`, `7d`) and a tokio task ticks at the parsed interval, enqueueing one `RaptorTriageMatch` at `Priority::Low` per (saved-as-triage tree × note in scope), driven by the existing `[suggestions.triage].scope` prefix filter. Settings UI row already exists from Sprint D. **Partial:** the spec's grammar is cron-shape (e.g. the spec example `"0 3 * * *"`); the runtime accepts only the duration grammar above and silently logs + disables anything else — a user copy-pasting the documented cron example gets no scheduled rerun. Cron-shape support is tracked as `cluster-editor-triage-scheduled-rerun-cron-syntax` |
| `cluster-editor-triage-scheduled-rerun-cron-syntax` | planned | accept the cron-shape grammar from the spec (`docs/cluster-editor.md` — `scheduled_rerun = "0 3 * * *"`) in `parse_rerun_interval` so the spec's documented example actually schedules. Likely pulls in a cron crate (or hand-rolled 5-field parser) and adapts the tokio ticker to fire on next-occurrence rather than fixed interval |
| `cluster-editor-triage-modified-rerun` | partial | `[suggestions.triage].modified_rerun` (bool, default `false`) + `.modified_rerun_cosine_guard` (float, default `0.15`) added in `core/src/config.rs`; eligible-key registered for both Vault and User scope; surfaced in the settings UI alongside the other triage controls (`app/src/panels/settings/mod.rs`). **Partial:** the runtime guard isn't yet wired — honoring the cosine threshold requires tracking each note's last-triaged embedding (a new `notes.last_triaged_embedding` column or equivalent), which is a follow-up. Today the on-save trigger fires unconditionally within `scope` per `cluster-editor-triage-on-save`; the modified-rerun config is forward-compat plumbing |
| `cluster-editor-triage-backfill` | planned | "Triage existing notes…" action runs a saved tree's classifier over a chosen scope (vault / folder / inbox) in one pass, so it tags/moves notes that already exist, not just new ones. Loops `triage_all_saved_trees` over the scope; emits a previewed, undoable review batch (every row forced `require_review`) into the tree-scoped batch-review pane (`cluster-editor-batch-review-pane`). No-op when a note is already placed where the classifier would put it. `Low` priority |
| `cluster-editor-triage-via-task-queue` | done | `cluster_triage_enqueue` host command submits one `TaskKind::RaptorTriageMatch { tree_id, source_path }` task per saved-as-triage tree at `Priority::Normal`. The synchronous classifier (`cluster-editor-triage-on-save`) is the on-save fast path; producers wanting queue visibility (CLI parity, scheduled rerun, manual "Re-triage this note") use the async path |
| `cluster-editor-row-primitive` | done | The shared row primitive lives in `app/src/sidebar/clusters/tree.rs`; both the sidebar (`app/src/sidebar/clusters/mod.rs`) and the expanded pane (`app/src/panels/cluster_review/mod.rs`) consume it. It carries: chevron + name (click-to-edit on clusters / click-to-open on leaves) + summary preview (click-to-edit) + members count + ↻ staleness badge + policy chip (popover: Tag/Move/Freeze/Clear) + outlier virtual node + Shift/Cmd/Ctrl-click selection (incl. shift-range via display-order anchor) + right-click context menu (Move to… / Split / Subcluster… / Merge children up / Summarize / Drop cluster / Send to outliers / Promote out of outliers…) + multi-select toolbar (Merge siblings / Drop / Summarize / Stage move to / Stage tag with / Clear) + drag-and-drop reparent with promote-to-top drop band and floating drag chip |
| `cluster-editor-not-for-trails-or-graph` | planned | the row primitive isn't reused by trails or the vault graph view (different data models) |
| `cluster-editor-renderer-reuse` | done | the egui force-graph widget (`app/src/widgets/force_graph.rs`, `app/src/panels/graph.rs`) is the shared renderer. Cluster editor's graph view is the first consumer; a future vault-wide graph view rides the same widget |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view` | done | `app/src/panels/cluster_graph.rs` (`ClusterGraph`, `show()`). Sub-sub-mode of `cluster-tree` toggled by `Tree`/`Graph` buttons in the pane toolbar. Renders the tree via the shared egui force-graph widget with policy-color / size / staleness / outline encoding |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-behavior` | done | umbrella — covered by the per-behavior slugs below (click, hover, multi-select, filter, selection-outline) |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-layout` | done | the layout engines are egui-agnostic in `hiker-graph` (the `hiker-render` submodule): `graph/src/tree.rs` (radial / vertical-tree / horizontal-tree pure-position layouts) + `graph/src/force.rs` (ForceAtlas2 + Barnes–Hut), wrapped by the egui adapter `widgets/graph-widgets/src/{graph_layouts,force_layout}.rs` (converts positions at the `egui::Vec2` boundary) and consumed by the cluster editor and the vault graph (`app/src/panels/graph.rs`). Radial is the default; layout id persisted in per-tree saved view state. The force-directed variant runs `LayoutParams { scaling_ratio: 100.0, outbound_attraction_distribution: false, degree_repulsion: true, .. }` — `outbound_attraction_distribution = false` is the ForceAtlas2 default and is what produces the "leaves sit in a ring around their hub at consistent radius" look; turning it on divides each edge's spring by the source mass, which lets hub leaves drift outward instead of settling at a fixed radius. `scaling_ratio = 100` keeps the settled graph spanning enough world units to render legibly at the default zoom. `tools/graph-snapshot/` is the headless harness for iterating on these tunings against synthetic graphs without launching the app |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-layout-extensible` | done | layout registry in `app/src/panels/cluster_graph.rs` keyed by layout id. Adding a layout = new function + registry entry; the renderer stays layout-agnostic (consumes only `x` / `y` on the node) |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-color-by-policy` | done | `app/src/panels/cluster_graph.rs` (policy-color encoding) — Move/Tag/Freeze/None each carry a distinct color; inherited (walked-up) policies render at 0.55 alpha; `⏸` glyph appended to the node label when `require_review` is set |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-size-by-members` | done | `app/src/panels/cluster_graph.rs` (member-size encoding) — `6 + log2(memberCount) * 2.5`, capped at 22; leaves render at fixed size 4 |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-label-style` | done | `app/src/widgets/force_graph.rs` (label rendering) paints a rounded white background (radius 3, padding 6×2) with a 1px `#c8c8c8` border behind every node label, so both cluster-tree and future vault-graph consumers pick it up |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-click-to-edit-policy` | done | Moved off plain left-click — the **right-click** path opens the Tag / Move / Freeze / Clear menu anchored at the pointer location. Plain left-click is reserved for select (and pin for leaves) so the row view's click semantics match the graph view. Wired through `app/src/panels/cluster_graph.rs`'s node right-click handling. Cluster / outlier-bucket nodes only; leaves have no per-node menu yet |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-hover-detail` | done | `app/src/panels/cluster_graph.rs` (hover handling) shows a tooltip with name + summary + member count + resolved policy; 500ms held-hover expands to leaf titles (capped at 10 + "and N more"). The tooltip tracks the cursor (12,12 offset, viewport-clamped) so it follows the pointer instead of pinning at the canvas corner; a `min-width` keeps sparse content from collapsing to a 1px line |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-policy-filter` | done | filter strip at the top of the canvas with All / Move / Tag / Freeze / No policy / Require review chips. Non-matching nodes render at 0.25 opacity rather than hidden, per spec |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-selection-outline` | done | Plain left-click single-selects (replaces prior selection); Shift+left-click extends/toggles for multi-select. Selected nodes render at `size + 2` with `outlineColor: var(--accent)`. A click on the canvas background clears both the selection and the pinned note-preview. Selection survives the row↔graph view switch via `sharedSelection`. Footer shows the count. **Note:** the graph→row write-back path exists (the graph view calls `onSelectionChanged` which feeds `sharedSelection`), but the row view doesn't yet consume `sharedSelection` on re-entry — selection persists across switches *from* the row view *to* graph, not yet the other direction; row-view consumer is a small follow-up |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-leaf-click-opens-note` | done | Single left-click on a leaf opens the note as a preview tab via the host-supplied `openNote` callback (`{ preview: true }`, matching sidebar / activity). Clusters route to right-click for the policy popover (per `cluster-editor-graph-view-click-to-edit-policy`); pin-on-click semantics removed in favor of `cluster-editor-graph-view-hover-preview-card` which tracks the card to the hovered node automatically |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-hover-preview-card` | done | `app/src/panels/cluster_graph.rs::paint_nodes` records a `HoveredNode { name, summary, leaf_path, screen_pos }` whenever the cursor is over any cluster or leaf node; the panel renders the in-canvas preview card via the shared `crate::panels::graph::paint_preview_card` helper when `state.show_preview` is on, anchored to `screen_pos` with quadrant-flip + canvas-clamp placement. Body source: leaves resolve `leaf_path` via `store.path_for_id(note_ref)` (with a fallback to using `note_ref` as a path string for the cluster-review embed where note_ref carries the vault-relative path directly) and the cached `selected_preview` is refreshed via `update_selection` on hover; the file body is passed through `panels::graph::skip_frontmatter` so the YAML block is dropped before truncation. Cluster nodes use their in-memory `summary`, falling back to whatever cached leaf body is still around when the summary is empty. Card style: light `#fafafa` background, `#c8cdd4` 1px border, dark text; title laid out with `painter.layout` against `inner.width()` so long basenames wrap inside the card. Vault graph (`app/src/panels/graph.rs::show`) shares the same paint helper and the same hover-to-update flow |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-no-reshape` | done | No drag-reshape on the canvas (only click + hover + pan/zoom). Footer note when selection is non-empty reminds the user that tree-shape edits stay in the row view |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-pan-zoom-keybinds` | done | The egui force-graph widget (`app/src/widgets/force_graph.rs`) handles pointer-drag pan / wheel zoom / pinch zoom. Chord ids `cluster-editor.graph-pan` / `.graph-zoom` / `.graph-zoom-pinch` are reserved in the spec for keybind-registry follow-up; defaults stay implicit at this layer |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-toggle` | done | View-mode selector lives inside the unified eye-icon "View options" menu on the pane toolbar (`cluster-editor-graph-view-view-menu`) — a "View as" radio with Tree / Graph / Markdown options, replacing the prior dedicated 3-button toggle strip. Sub-sub-mode of `cluster-tree`, not a new `BufferMode`. Switching destroys+remounts the canvas (no orphan WebGL refs); selection survives |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-view-menu` | done | Single eye-icon button on the pane's pinned toolbar (`app/src/panels/cluster_review/mod.rs`, always visible — not gated on the active variant) opens a unified popover with: (a) a "View as" radio (Tree / Graph / Markdown — replaces the prior 3-button toggle strip on the toolbar); (b) in tree mode, **Expand all** / **Collapse all** verbs over the pane-local expanded set (every cluster / outlier-bucket node added on expand-all; cleared on collapse-all); (c) in graph mode, the graph-specific items (leaves / layout radios + show-outliers / fit / reset / note-preview toggle) folded in. Glyph matches the editor toolbar's view-options icon. The view-mode radio refreshes the menu in place. Same menu primitive as the editor's view-options menu |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-leaf-visibility` | done | View-menu radio: `Hide leaves` / `Auto (LOD)` (default — hidden when camera ratio ≥ 1.2) / `Show all leaves`. Persisted in saved view state |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-show-outliers` | done | View-menu toggle, default on. When off, the outlier-bucket node and its descendants are dropped from the renderer (distinct from build-time `include_outliers` which controls whether they're generated). Persisted |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-reset-fit` | done | View-menu `Fit to view` animates the camera to frame the graph; `Reset view` snaps the camera to its default center + zoom. Camera state persisted (debounced 400ms) to saved view state |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-outlier-disconnected` | done | the outlier-bucket layout in `app/src/panels/cluster_graph.rs` places the outlier bucket floating off to the lower-right and drops the parent edge whose parent is an outlier bucket, so it renders disconnected. Label `Outliers (N)` |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-saved-view-state` | done | Per-tree state (layout id, leaf visibility, show-outliers, policy filter, camera) persisted in `localStorage` under `hiker.clusterEditor.graphView.<treeId>`. Spec explicitly allows this or a `cluster_trees.view_state` column; localStorage is the simpler of the two for UI-only state and adds zero schema migration |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-lazy-load` | done | the graph view (`app/src/panels/cluster_graph.rs`) only builds its layout + renderer state when the user toggles to graph view; the egui force-graph widget is a native dependency so there's no separate bundle to lazy-load |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-not-vault-graph` | done | The cluster-tree graph view (`app/src/panels/cluster_graph.rs`) consumes the shared egui force-graph widget but its data plumbing (cluster trees + policies) is entirely separate from any future vault-wide graph view (notes + wikilinks). Different panels, shared widget primitive only |
| `cluster-editor-tree-diff-view` | planned | deferred — diff between two cluster trees (e.g. fresh build vs. saved triage) so user can see what shifted |
| `cluster-editor-recluster-subtree` | done | UI now routes through the clustering review tab. Row-menu "Recluster subtree…" entry calls `openClusterReviewTab({ kind: "recluster-subtree", treeId, nodeId, nodeName })`; the tab runs the structural pass via `cluster_run_structural` (recluster_target arm) and on Confirm calls `cluster_op_recluster_subtree_from_built` which replaces the subtree the same shape as the legacy `cluster_op_recluster_subtree` (records `recluster-subtree` history row via `Trees::record_recluster_subtree`, namespaced ids `recluster-<node>-<id>`, leaves re-parent through `reparent_many`, selected node row preserved, `carry_policies_down` copies the resolved policy onto direct children) and submits per-cluster `RaptorSummarize` tasks for non-user-renamed nodes. Legacy `cluster_op_recluster_subtree` command is preserved (unused by UI) for tests / CLI |
| `cluster-editor-recluster-subtree-policy-loss` | done | `cluster_op_recluster_subtree` walks up from the selected node to the root to compute the *resolved* policy (first explicit ancestor's policy wins, including the selected node's own). When the prompt's "Carry policies down from selected node" checkbox is checked (default-off per spec), that resolved policy is copied as an explicit policy onto every new direct child of the selected node. The `recluster-subtree` history row snapshots `prior_subtree` with each descendant's full row shape including its `policy` JSON — `undo_recluster_subtree` re-inserts them via `restore_node_row`, so undo restores the full prior subtree including policies exactly. Carried policy also flows onto the args JSON so redo replays the same carried-policy state |
| `cluster-editor-recluster-subtree-placement-decoupled` | done | the op is structural only — it never calls `core::staging` and never invokes `move_note` against the vault, so no filesystem changes happen as a side effect of reclustering. The selected node's `cluster_nodes` subtree changes shape; the surviving leaves' `note_ref` columns are untouched, so the notes' on-disk paths stay where they were. Future triage classifications fired through `cluster-place-beam-descent` use the new centroids; the existing Apply path (per `cluster-editor-apply-action`) is the explicit way to project the new structure onto disk via `move_note` staging rows. No code change beyond what `cluster-editor-recluster-subtree` already enforces — this slug is the *absence* of the obvious mistake, captured by ensuring the op only mutates `cluster_nodes` |
| `suggestions-apply-cmd` | done | `core/src/suggest.rs::apply_tree(trees, tree_id, vault, store, log, history)` is the shared apply mechanic — now op-log-backed: `Move` leaves stage one cross-document reorg batch of pending `Rename` ops (`op-log-reorg-batch`, author `auto:cluster`), `Tag` leaves stage one pending content op each via `op_writes::stage_auto_content`. Nothing reaches disk until the user accepts in the batch-review pane. The UI path satisfies the slug per `docs/cluster-editor.md`'s Apply flow; the CLI binary lands as the sub-slug `suggestions-apply-cmd-cli` |
| `suggestions-apply-cmd-cli` | planned | sub-slug of `suggestions-apply-cmd` — `hiker suggest apply <tree-id>` CLI surface with `--interactive` / `--accept-all` / `--dry-run`. `cli/src/main.rs` is currently a 3-line stub; this slug covers wiring it onto `core::suggest::apply_tree` |
| `suggestions-rejection-history` | partial | `core/src/suggest.rs::RejectionHistory` owns `.hiker/suggestion-history.json` — per-`(fingerprint, note_path, action)` rows with a 90-day TTL (GC on every write). Cluster-editor batch-review pane calls `cluster_record_rejection` alongside `staging_reject` so the log is populated on every user reject; `apply_tree` consults the log and skips already-rejected rows. **Partial:** the fingerprint is Sprint C's coarse form (`hash(parent_cluster_name + note_path + action)`); the spec's member-set Jaccard recovery is a follow-up. TTL configurability is also deferred |
| `suggestions-mode-move` | done | `core/src/suggest.rs` stages a pending `Rename` op per move (`target_path = <folder>/<basename>`), authored `auto:cluster`, as part of a reorg batch. Skips no-op moves (target == source). Accept applies the `Rename` through the op log (`op_writes::flip_batch_status`), repointing `doc-index.db` + `meta.path`; the filesystem move stays the caller's concern downstream of `core::vault::move_note` |
| `suggestions-mode-tag` | done | `core/src/suggest.rs::merge_tag_into_frontmatter` pre-computes the file content with the tag appended (idempotent — skipped if already present) under the configured `tag_field` (default `hiker.suggested_tags` per spec). The proposal carries `action = "apply_tag"`, the new content, `source_hash` for drift safety, and `metadata.tag_slug` / `tag_field`. Accept path is the existing content-write path (no new code) |
| `suggestions-tag-field-configurable` | planned | `[suggestions] tag_field` config; default `hiker.suggested_tags`, can be set to `tags` to use the regular list |
| `triage-staging-proposals` | done | now op-log-backed: `core::suggest::triage_match` stages a pending `Rename` (Move policy) or pending content op (Tag policy) authored `auto:triage` via `op_writes`, applied immediately when no review is required. `Freeze` policies and no-op (target == source) moves skip without emitting an op. Out-of-scope sources (per `[triage].scope`) also skip pre-emission |
| `triage-review-required` | done | `core/src/suggest.rs::triage_match` computes `effective_requires_review = policy.require_review || opts.review_required || author_class == Agent` and stamps it on `metadata.require_review`. The accept-side reads the flag to decide whether to auto-accept on insert vs hold pending. `[triage].review_required` config key (default `true`) is registered in `ELIGIBLE_USER` + `ELIGIBLE_VAULT` (`core/src/config.rs`) and surfaced in the Settings UI's "Triage" card. The `author = "auto:triage"` stamp on the auto-accepted `core::changes` row is `AUTHOR_AUTO_TRIAGE` (per `triage-author-class`) — consumed by the accept-time auto-accept path when the worker lands |
| `triage-author-class` | partial | Engine supports User vs Agent: `core/src/suggest.rs::NoteAuthorClass { User, Agent }` + `TriageInput.author_class` flow into `effective_requires_review = policy.require_review || opts.review_required || author_class == Agent`, so an Agent classification reliably forces pending. **Gap:** no caller in Sprint D actually emits `Agent` against a real agent write. The on-save watcher spawn (the host ~line 1242) hardcodes `User`; `cluster_triage_run` accepts a string override but the only frontend caller passes nothing; `cluster_triage_enqueue` doesn't even carry the hint on its `RaptorTriageMatch` payload (consumer in `DirectWorkerHandlers::try_handle` also passes `User`). The agent-author detection mechanism (watcher event source vs staging-history recent-agent-write lookup vs an explicit producer-side hint on the task variant) lands with the auto-accept worker / cancellable queue. The `AUTHOR_AUTO_TRIAGE` constant ("auto:triage") is reserved on the auto-accept path; the user-accept path keeps `author = "user"` per `op-log.md` |
| `triage-auto-undo-toast` | planned | toast + 10s Undo fires from the auto-accept path; Undo logs to rejection history |

## Trails (trails.md)

## Observability (observability.md)

v1 slice is just "init `tracing` and write to a file." Spans, in-app viewer, and the frontend bridge are deferred until file logs stop answering questions.

### v1

| Slug | Status | Notes |
| ---- | ------ | ----- |
| `obs-tracing-baseline` | done | `core/src/observability.rs` (`init_tracing`); the host (`pick_vault` calls it). All prior `eprintln!` in `core::indexer` / `core::store` / `core::watcher` converted to `tracing::{debug,info,warn,error}!` |
| `obs-log-files` | done | `core/src/observability.rs` (`init_tracing`) — file layer writes `<vault>/.hiker/logs/hiker.log` alongside the stderr layer |
| `obs-log-rotation` | done | `core/src/observability.rs` — `tracing_appender::rolling::Builder` with `Rotation::DAILY` + `max_log_files(7)` |
| `obs-error-context` | done | pattern applied throughout core — e.g. `core/src/indexer.rs` upsert err branch uses `error!(error = %e, path = %rel_path, ...)`; no string-interpolated context |
| `obs-no-content` | done | discipline-only; v1 events log paths/reasons/counts only, never note body text. Documented at the top of `core/src/observability.rs` |
| `obs-no-secrets` | done | discipline-only; nothing in v1 logs auth tokens or API keys (no such config exists yet). Documented at the top of `core/src/observability.rs` |

### Deferred (post-v1)

| Slug | Status | Notes |
| ---- | ------ | ----- |
| `obs-spans-pipeline` | planned | spans wrap pipeline stages, not individual fn calls; deferred — adopt when flat events get hard to correlate |
| `obs-env-filter` | planned | `HIKER_LOG` env var drives `EnvFilter`; defaults `info,hiker=debug`; deferred — v1 hardcodes the default |
| `obs-instrument-watcher` | planned | one span per debounced event; raw events at `trace!` only; deferred behind `obs-spans-pipeline` |

## Search (search.md)

v2 milestone. Vault-wide hybrid search (lexical FTS5 + semantic) hosted in the repurposed discovery panel; type-ahead with debounce + epoch-cancel; engine traits so tantivy can swap in later. Phases 1–4 landed: backend foundation, panel restructure + persistence, type-ahead + result rendering + click-to-chunk, Ctrl-Space focus + arrow/Enter/Tab/Esc nav. The deferred slugs at the bottom of the table are next; they're scoped follow-ups, not v2 blockers.

| Slug | Status | Notes |
| ---- | ------ | ----- |

| `search-bar-input` | done | `app/src/panels/search.rs` (search input) |
| `search-mode-toggles` | done | `app/src/panels/search.rs` (semantic/lexical toggles) — S/L pills next to the input; pressed state same as existing toolbar buttons |
| `search-modes-both-off-disabled` | done | `app/src/panels/search.rs` (input disabled state) — both toggles off → input disabled + placeholder swaps to "Enable Semantic or Lexical to search" |
| `search-mode-state-persisted` | done | `core/src/config.rs` (`SearchConfig`, `SearchModesConfig` defaults true/true) + eligible-key set entries `search.modes.{semantic,lexical}`; `app/src/panels/search.rs` seeds from settings on vault open and persists every flip |
| `search-typeahead-debounce` | done | `app/src/panels/search.rs` — 250ms debounce + monotonically-increasing search epoch; stale responses dropped before render. Empty query short-circuits without scheduling and bumps the epoch so any in-flight call drops |
| `search-empty-collapses-results` | done | `app/src/panels/search.rs` (section visibility) — non-empty query reveals the search section, empty hides it; related section keeps the panel content as before |

| `search-loading-shimmer` | done | `app/src/panels/search.rs` (in-flight spinner) — minimal "…" spinner shown while a debounced query is in flight; styling can be upgraded later |

| `search-result-click-opens-chunk` | done | `app/src/panels/search.rs` (open-hit handler) — clicking a result row opens the file then scrolls the editor (`editor-view` `ViewState`) to the chunk's `byte_start` |
| `search-result-budget` | done | `core/src/search.rs` — `PER_BACKEND_TOP_K = 25`, `FUSED_TOP_K = 20`; configurability deferred to MCP needs |
| `search-keybind-ctrl-space` | done | `app/src/keybinds.rs` (`search.focusInput` / `Ctrl-Space`) — registered so it wins over completion inside the editor, plus a cross-pane handler for the global case (checks `ctrlKey && !metaKey`, so Cmd-Space on macOS stays Spotlight). Both focus the search input, which expands the panel if collapsed and selects existing input contents. The keybind registry doesn't yet have a `scope` field — the global half lives outside the registry until that refactor lands |
| `search-keyboard-nav` | done | `app/src/panels/search.rs` + `app/src/panels/related.rs` (list keyboard handling) — ↑/↓ within a list, vertical wrap between Search-bottom ↔ Related-top only; Enter triggers the row's open action; Tab steps one row per list so input → search → related → out flows naturally; Esc in the input clears the query (or blurs if already empty), Esc on a row refocuses the input. ↓ from the input jumps to the first available result row |
| `search-engine-trait` | done | `core/src/search.rs` — `LexicalEngine` + `SemanticEngine` traits with concrete impls in same file; tantivy swap-point preserved |
| `search-fts5-lexical` | done | `core/src/search.rs` (`Fts5LexicalEngine`) |
| `search-fts5-schema` | done | `core/src/store.rs` (`ensure_schema`) — contentless `chunks_fts` + sync triggers on `chunks` (insert/update/delete); schema bumped (now `SCHEMA_VERSION = 10` after later feature migrations) |
| `search-fts5-bm25-snippet` | done | `core/src/search.rs` (`Fts5LexicalEngine::query`) — `ORDER BY bm25` + `snippet(chunks_fts, 0, '<mark>', '</mark>', '…', 32)`; BM25 sign-flipped so higher = better matches the semantic side |
| `search-semantic-existing-vecs` | done | `core/src/search.rs` (`VecSemanticEngine`) — thin wrapper over `Store::knn_chunks_on` |
| `search-query-embed-spawn-blocking` | done | `app/src/search/mod.rs::fire_query` hands the whole fired query to `tokio::task::spawn_blocking` — query-string embed (via `indexer.embedder()`, the slow ONNX step) plus the lexical/vector core query, the federated-ZIM searches, and the recency/ext post-filters all run off the UI thread. The task ships a `SearchOutcome` (tagged with `query_epoch`) over an `mpsc` channel; `drain_results` applies it each frame and drops stale epochs (superseded by newer typing), so typing never blocks. A `Spinner` + "Searching…" hint shows while in flight. The `.zim` registry (`panels/zim.rs`) is a global `Mutex<ZimRegistry>` (was thread-local) so `federated_search`/`federated_fulltext_search` are callable from the worker. Runs alongside the 250ms `search-typeahead-debounce`. Per-section incremental render (lexical painting before semantic) is not split out — one combined outcome lands at once |
| `search-rrf-fusion` | done | `core/src/search.rs` (`rrf_fuse`) — k=60, applied when both modes on; group-by-note happens before fuse |
| `search-rebuild-on-schema-bump` | done | covered by `store-version-fail-loud`: opening a v2 db with this binary aborts with a version-mismatch error; user runs the existing reindex flow |
| `search-vault-scope-only` | done | `core/src/search.rs` — engine queries hit every non-skipped chunk in the vault; no scope filter; folder/tag/lifecycle filters stay deferred per spec |
| `search-cmd` | done | `core/src/search.rs` returning `SearchResponse { epoch, lexical_hits, semantic_hits, fused }`; both-modes-off / empty-query / model-not-ready short-circuit to empty buckets without erroring |
| `search-mode-options-menu` | done | `app/src/panels/search.rs` (lexical / semantic options menus, right-click on the mode toggles; slider / number / radio rows). Left-click still flips on/off; right-click opens the mode-specific popover anchored under the button |
| `search-lexical-options` | done | `core/src/config.rs` (`SearchLexicalConfig`, `ELIGIBLE_VAULT` entries `search.lexical.*`); `core/src/search.rs` (`LexicalOpts`); `app/src/panels/search.rs` (lexical options menu) — flips persist via `settings-write-back` to `search.lexical.*` and rerun the in-flight query |
| `search-lexical-case-sensitive` | done | `core/src/search.rs` (`Fts5LexicalEngine::query` post-filter via `chunk_contains` over `c.text`) — pulled alongside the snippet so no second fetch needed |
| `search-lexical-diacritic-sensitive` | done | `core/src/search.rs` (same `chunk_contains` post-filter, byte-level substring match against `c.text`) — diacritic-strict falls out of literal substring naturally; an NFD-aware variant can swap in if a user hits the case-sensitive-but-diacritic-insensitive cell |
| `search-lexical-prefix-match` | done | `core/src/search.rs` (`build_match_string` rewrites each whitespace token to `token*` before the FTS5 `MATCH`) — phrase mode wins when both flags are set, FTS5 ignores `*` inside a quoted phrase |
| `search-lexical-phrase-mode` | done | `core/src/search.rs` (`build_match_string` wraps the trimmed query in double quotes; embedded `"` is doubled per FTS5 string-literal rules) |

## LLM (llm.md)

All deferred. Lands with the v3.5 ACP-client + bundled-agent milestone in `design.md` build order. Architectural decisions (ACP-only, three feature types, ToS posture, etc.) live in the spec; the slugs below are concrete implementable features.

| Slug | Status | Notes |
| ---- | ------ | ----- |
| `llm-core-module` | done | `core/src/llm.rs` — `LlmClient` trait (`chat` / `chat_stream`) + `GraniteLlmClient` wrapping graniet/`llm`; `ProviderConfig` carries backend/model/api-key-env/base-url/limits; module discipline holds (only place the `llm` crate is imported). `MockLlmClient` for downstream tests. TOML loader for `llm-providers-config` is the next slug |
| `llm-providers-config` | done | `core/src/config.rs` — `[llm]` section in the standard hiker config (per-vault `vault/.hiker/config.toml` with user-scope fallback, deep-merged like every other section): top-level `enabled` (reserved for `llm-disable-mode`), `[llm.provider]` (backend / model / api_key_env / api_key / base_url), `[llm.limits]` (max_tokens / timeout_secs), `[llm.agent]` (iteration_cap / tool_timeout_secs), `[llm.audit] log_full_prompt`. Strict-load validates the section. `core::llm::provider_config_from` + `GraniteLlmClient::from_config` build a usable client from the loaded section. API keys come from one of two sources, in precedence order: literal `[llm.provider].api_key` (user-scope TOML only — eligibility list refuses vault-scope writes so a synced vault can't carry the secret) takes precedence over `api_key_env` when set. The settings pane hides the `api_key` row whenever the section's scope toggle is at `[Vault]`. Empty + empty = no key on the builder (correct for local Ollama). |
| `llm-basic-agent-loop` | done | `core/src/agent.rs` — `run_turn(input, client, dispatcher, cfg, events_tx, cancel)` runs one user-message → terminal-response cycle. `LlmClient` extended with `chat_with_tools` (returns `ToolChatResponse` carrying text + `Vec<ToolCall>`); message history carries `tool_calls` / `tool_results` so multi-turn tool-use is coherent. Tool dispatch goes through the injected `ToolDispatcher` trait — the MCP wiring still lives in `agent-tool-routing-via-mcp` (separate slug). 6 unit tests cover terminal turn, tool-use turn, cap-hit, tool timeout, tool failure, and cancel. |
| `agent-tool-routing-via-mcp` | done | `mcp-server/src/agent_bridge.rs::McpAgentDispatcher` implements `core::agent::ToolDispatcher` against an `Arc<HikerHandler>` exposed via `McpServerHandle::agent_handler()`. `HikerHandler::dispatch_tool(name, args_json)` is the new in-process entry — same `_inner()` helpers the rmcp tools call, same audit log, same error model. `agent_tool_defs()` advertises the seven tools with `schemars`-derived JSON Schemas; the host's chat module injects this dispatcher into `run_turn`. |
| `agent-event-stream-shape` | done | `core/src/agent.rs::AgentEvent` covers the 11 spec variants (`ToolCallArgsDelta` added with the streaming variant). Emitted on `tokio::mpsc::Sender<AgentEvent>` from `core::agent`; the host re-emits each on the chat events channel; `app/src/chat/render.rs` dispatches by `kind` to render text deltas, tool-call cards, the Continue/Stop row, errors, and finish-reason rows. |
| `agent-chat-command-surface` | done | Backend: the host's chat module — `chat_send` / `chat_continue` / `chat_stop` / `chat_cancel` commands; `VaultSession::chat: Arc<ChatRegistry>` holds the spec's `Arc<Mutex<HashMap<TurnId, TurnState>>>` shape (history, tools, system prompt, `StopSignal`, `in_flight` flag, finish flag). Each `chat_send` / `chat_continue` spawns a per-turn tokio task (refused under the registry lock if `in_flight`) that drives `run_turn`, forwards each event through a tiny event-pump task, and writes the new history back so a follow-up resume sees the latest state; an RAII guard wrapping the spawned task clears `in_flight` (and GCs the registry on terminal finish) regardless of how the task exits, so a panic mid-loop can't leak the entry. `chat_continue` re-invokes `run_turn` directly with `user_message: None` per the spec — the cap reset falls out because `run_turn`'s cap is per-call, no empty user message is appended. `chat_stop` fires `StopSignal::user_halt` (→ `FinishReason::UserHalted`); `chat_cancel` fires `StopSignal::cancel` (→ `FinishReason::Cancelled`). Frontend wired up in `app/src/chat/render.rs` (+ `send.rs`) — see `chat-panel-*` rows. |
| `agent-iteration-cap-prompt` | done | core-side: `run_turn` returns `FinishReason::CapHit`, emits `AgentEvent::IterationCapHit`. UI-side: `app/src/chat/render.rs` renders the spec's `"Agent has made N tool calls — [Continue] [Stop]"` row; Continue invokes `chat_continue(turn_id)` (which re-enters `run_turn` with the same accumulated history and a fresh cap budget — exactly "resets to 10 more"); Stop invokes `chat_stop(turn_id)`. |
| `agent-tool-call-timeout` | done | `core/src/agent.rs::dispatch_with_timeout` wraps each `ToolDispatcher::dispatch` call in `tokio::time::timeout` using `cfg.tool_timeout_secs` (default 30 from `[llm.agent] tool_timeout_secs`). On timeout: emits a `ToolResult { ok: false, summary: "tool timed out" }` event and feeds the same shape into history so the model can recover. Never bubbles as a turn-killing error. Cancellation is interleaved via `tokio::select!` so a `chat_cancel` mid-dispatch unblocks the wait without leaking the dispatcher task to its natural completion. |
| `llm-acp-client-optional` | done | `core/src/acp.rs:1`, `core/src/config.rs:137`, the host chat module, `app/src/panels/settings/mod.rs` | `core::acp` module spawns external ACP agent process, creates session with hiker MCP server auto-attached via `McpServer::Http`, translates ACP events to `AgentEvent`. `[acp].agent` config key ("none" / "claude-code" / "goose"), settings pane dropdown. `chat_send`/`chat_continue` branch to `spawn_acp_turn()` when agent != "none". Session resume via ACP's `session/load` not yet supported — agent starts fresh each turn |
| `llm-context-injection` | planned | when hiker has high-confidence relevant context for an interactive turn, inject it as Embedded Resource (ACP) or in-prompt context (basic agent loop) |
| `llm-features-disable-entirely` | planned | master AI gate: `[llm] enabled = false` hides the chat panel + every AI-touching surface (note-mutations menu LLM entries, AI commands in palette + right-click menus, LLM task-queue lane), no-ops background + fan-out features, closes any open `agent` tab. MCP server stays available; deterministic pipelines (indexing/embedding/search/clustering build/wikilinks/op-log/sync) keep running. User-scope default; per-vault override via section scope toggle. Live-applied |
| `llm-feature-debounce` | planned | 1–2s coalesce window for save-driven LLM features so save bursts → one prompt |
| `llm-prompts-file-store` | done | `core/src/prompts.rs` — two-tier loader: user `~/.config/hiker/prompts/<feature>.md` (auto-created from bundled defaults on first run via `ensure_user_default`), vault `vault/.hiker/prompts/<feature>.md` (vault wins). `Prompts::load(vault_root)` produces an in-memory store; `bundled_defaults()` registers the v3.5 features (currently `chat_system` for the basic agent loop's system prompt). `the host's `prepare_for_turn`` uses it to render the system prompt with `{{vault_name}}` substituted. |
| `llm-prompts-mustache-templating` | done | `core/src/prompts.rs::substitute` — `{{var}}` substitution (whitespace-tolerant). Unknown placeholders pass through verbatim rather than blanking, so a partially-customized prompt doesn't silently lose data. Each shipped default starts with an HTML-comment header naming its placeholders. |
| `llm-prompts-staleness-on-upgrade` | done | `core/src/prompts.rs::Prompts::staleness` — sidecar `<feature>.default.sha` next to each user-scope prompt records the bundled default's blake3 hash at first-write. Bumping the bundled default upstream surfaces as a stale-feature name from `staleness()`; the user file is never clobbered. the host (`open_vault_at_inner`) calls `staleness()` once per session and emits both a `tracing::warn!` and an `AgentLog` row (`surface = "core::agent"`, `status = "stale_prompt"`) per stale feature, so the future Prompts-tab can read either surface. |

| `llm-audit-log` | done | `core/src/audit.rs` — `AgentLog` JSONL writer at `<vault>/.hiker/agent-log/<YYYY-MM-DD>.jsonl` with daily rotation, single mutex per writer, content-blind once `record` is called. `AuditEntry` carries `surface` (`core::agent` / `core::llm` / `core::acp` / `mcp-tool-call`), `feature`, `status`, optional `error`, optional `turn_id` / `step_id`, and free-form `details` so debugging trails can correlate panel events with audit rows. Wired three places: `core::agent::run_turn` writes one row per step (status ok / error, tool-call count, finish reason; full text only when `log_full_prompt = true`); `mcp-server/src/audit.rs` is now a thin wrapper over the shared writer carrying the existing `[mcp.audit] log_full_input` redaction policy with `surface = "mcp-tool-call"`; the host records `stale_prompt` rows for `Prompts::staleness` hits. The shared writer means `[mcp.audit] log_full_input` and `[llm.audit] log_full_prompt` land their rows in the same daily file. Background / fan-out / ACP surfaces will plug in by passing `Some(AgentAudit { ... })` (or constructing their own `AuditEntry`) when those slugs land. |

Same column as search/related, the same discovery toggle flips the whole panel; when LLM is disabled, the chat region and handle are removed so sections fill the panel. |
| `chat-panel-detached-scroll` | done | `#discovery-sections` and `.chat-transcript` each have their own `overflow-y: auto` + `min-height: 0`, so the sections region and chat transcript scroll independently. The drag-handle divider visually separates them; scrolling the transcript doesn't move search results out of view, and vice versa. |

A one-time migration (`core::sessions::migrate_to_chats_dir`, run from `app/src/bootstrap.rs`) relocates legacy `.hiker/sessions/` content into `chats/` |
| `chat-session-tree-visibility` | done | sessions write to the visible `<chats_dir>/` folder (`core/src/sessions.rs`) so they show in the Files tree as ordinary notes; the `vault.show_sessions_in_tree` toggle + `ELIGIBLE_VAULT` entry + settings row + `.hiker/sessions/` watcher carve-out are all removed | Sessions are ordinary visible notes in `chats/` — shown in the Files tree, grouped in Vault mode by provenance with a title+date label (`vault-view-source-groups`); no hide/reveal toggle. Vault-mode provenance grouping (`vault-view-source-groups`) tracks separately |
| `chat-session-resume-latest` | partial | `app/src/chat/session.rs::discover` runs at first chat-surface render over `core::sessions::list` (which scans the visible `chats/` folder + its `imported/` subfolder), hydrates each `ChatSession` via `parse_session`, and adopts the newest as active. New session opts out; older re-open rides `chat-session-list-ui` | Scans the visible `chats/` folder (default; `[chat] chats_dir`). GAP — resume happens lazily on first chat-surface open, not eagerly at vault open |
| `chat-session-list-ui` | partial | compact dropdown left of the chat resize handle (`app/src/chat/render.rs`); `app/src/chat/session.rs::discover` enumerates the visible `chats/` folder (+ `imported/`) with first-user-preview; switching hydrates via `session::set_active`; per-row trash icon ships under `chat-session-trash` | Enumerates the visible `chats/` folder (default; `[chat] chats_dir`). GAP — no turn-count column; preview-only rows |
| `chat-session-trash` | done | `the host's `chat_session_delete`` resolves the session file's vault-relative path (registry-cached, falls back to `core::sessions::list_sessions`), routes through `core::ops::delete` so the move-to-`.hiker/trash/` + index cascade happen once, then drops the in-memory entry via `ChatRegistry::forget`. Trash icon per dropdown row (`app/src/chat/render.rs`). Restore + permanent delete reuse the regular trash-bin commands | path-agnostic (resolves the session path at call time), so it works unchanged once sessions move to `chats/` — restore lands the file back at its `chats/` path |
| `chat-active-note-context-injection` | done | `app/src/chat/render.rs` (`active_context_label` + the send path) prepends `[active note: <path>]` for a focused editable `Editor{Vault}` note or `[active board: <path>]` for a focused `Board` tab, when the draft has no `@`-mention; read-only previews (snapshot/staging/trash) and non-content app-page tabs are skipped. Path reference only — the agent fetches content via `get_note`/`board_get` (per `llm.md`, deliberately not live buffer text). ACP Embedded-Resource path lands with `core::acp` |
| `chat-input-at-mentions` | done | `app/src/chat/send.rs` (`@`-token parser + popover + context-block composition + send wiring), `the host's `ChatContextBlock`` + `spawn_turn_task` injection loop | umbrella for explicit-context tokens. Frontend parses `@selection` / `@<rel-path>`, resolves them at submit time (`chat_resolve_at_note` for notes, the live selection for the highlight), composes the active note + explicit mentions into a single ordered + de-duplicated context-blocks list, and rides the new `chat_send` parameter into `spawn_turn_task` which appends one synthetic user-context message per block. ACP path lands its Embedded Resource ContentBlock shape when `core::acp` does |
| `chat-input-at-selection` | done | `app/src/chat/send.rs` (`selection` token kind + active-selection capture: rel-path + 1-based line range, `null` for empty / preview buffers; selection block never de-duped) | the captured selection rides as a `selection`-kind block carrying source rel-path + `L<a>-L<b>` line range + selected text. Empty / no-editor selection at submit time toasts "@selection has no selected text" and aborts the send so the user can fix the input |
| `chat-input-at-note` | done | `app/src/chat/send.rs` (`note` token kind with rel-path-without-extension), `the host's `chat_resolve_at_note`` (probes `.md` / `.markdown` / `.txt`) | resolves `@<rel-path-without-extension>` against the vault at submit time; on miss the backend error ("note not found: <rel>") flows through the toast and aborts the send |
| `chat-input-at-mentions-dedup` | done | `app/src/chat/send.rs` (seen-rel-paths set; first-seen wins; selection never de-duped) | active-note + every `@<rel-path>` collapse to one block per unique rel-path (including the active-note auto-inject). `@selection` is excluded from the de-dup set since the slice is the point. Implementation drops subsequent dupes outright per the implementation plan; the spec's "see prior block for `<path>`" refinement is equivalent for the model and can be added later if needed |
| `chat-input-at-autocomplete` | done | `app/src/chat/send.rs` (`@`-mention autocomplete popover) | popover anchors above the chat input inside the discovery panel. Arrow Up/Down move the highlighted row, Enter / Tab accept, Escape dismisses (intercepts the Enter-to-send chain when open). `selection` row is greyed when the active editor has no non-empty selection. Backspace at the tail of an inserted token deletes the whole token range as a single edit. Suppressed inside fenced code blocks (single-line and multi-line) and after a `\@` escape |
| `chat-input-at-autocomplete-cmd` | done | `core/src/store.rs::Store::at_autocomplete` + `AtSuggestion` DTO | empty prefix → top-N notes by `last_accessed_at DESC` (NULLs last); non-empty → case-insensitive basename substring with prefix-match-quality + recency ranking; skipped rows excluded; `rel_path` returned with the indexable extension stripped to match the token format. The `selection` synthetic entry is added frontend-side. 2 unit tests in `core/src/store.rs` cover ordering / disambiguation / skipped-row exclusion |
| `chat-input-at-folder` | planned | — | deferred — `@<folder>/` injects every note under the folder with a context-budget guard |
| `chat-input-at-tag` | planned | — | deferred — `@<tag>` injects every note carrying the named frontmatter tag; lands once tags are real |
| `chat-input-at-search` | planned | — | deferred — `@search:<query>` injects the top-N search results for an inline query |
| `chat-input-at-mention-chips` | planned | — | deferred — visual chip rendering of resolved mentions in the input box (cosmetic upgrade over plain `@token` text) |
| `chat-panel-stop-button` | done | `app/src/chat/render.rs` swaps the send button's icon (stop glyph), tooltip, and label whenever a turn is in flight; submit + Enter route through the stop path (`chat_stop(session_id, turn_id)`) instead of send while busy. Reverting to the Send affordance happens on `TurnFinished` / `IterationCapHit` / `error`, so cap-hit rows fall back to their existing Continue/Stop buttons. |
| `chat-panel-thinking-indicator` | done | `app/src/chat/render.rs` (thinking-indicator lifecycle). Three dots pulse on a staggered cycle. Inserted on send / after a tool result; removed on the first text delta / tool-call start of the upcoming step, on cap-hit, on `TurnFinished`, on Error, and on hydrate / reset. The spec asked for a "Thinking…" spinner; we substituted the pulsing-dots shape because it reads better in the compact panel and matches the rest of the UI's visual language |
| `chat-panel-markdown-render` | done | `app/src/chat/render.rs` renders each assistant bubble through the editor crates (`editor-md` `markdown_decorations()` over the editor-egui `Widget`) so headings/lists/inline+fenced code/emphasis/links render the same as the editor. The bubble view is read-only and only structure the renderer itself emits is shown — no raw HTML pass-through. In-vault link clicks (`hiker://note/<rel>` or bare relative paths) route through the open-note-link path; external `http(s)`/`file` links open externally; other schemes are inert. The editor chrome (background, padding, focus outline, caret) is stripped so the bubble blends in. |
| `chat-panel-note-link-render` | done | `app/src/chat/render.rs` — link clicks inside the per-bubble view resolve the enclosing link node, extract the URL, and route in-vault targets (bare relative paths or `hiker://note/<rel>`) through the open-note-link path (which respects `file-switch-guard-dirty`). External `http(s)://` / `file://` links open externally. Other schemes are inert. The system-prompt advertise-`hiker://note/<rel>` half lives in `core/prompts/chat_system.md` once that prompt is updated; the click handler accepts both forms today. |
| `chat-panel-tool-call-collapsible` | done | `app/src/chat/render.rs` — tool-call cards render in a one-line `▸ <name>(<short args>) <glyph> — <result>` shape; click expands to a pretty-printed JSON view with a Copy button. The short-args summary walks the parsed JSON to build a deterministic ≤80-char summary (first 1–2 fields, value-truncated); falls back to a whitespace-collapsed slice when the args don't parse cleanly. Per-card expanded state, not persisted. Errored calls (`ok = false`) stay collapsed by default; the ✗ glyph + result summary is enough at a glance. |
| `chat-tool-call-opens-touched-note` | planned | — | tool-call card header (click target separate from the expand-chevron) opens the touched note in the editor for note-touching tool variants (`get_note`, `write_note`, `set_frontmatter`, `apply_tag`, `remove_tag`, future `modify_note`). Resolves the rel-path from the tool result (or args fallback); for multi-note touches, header-click opens a picker. Honors `file-switch-guard-dirty`. Pairs with the trail-from-llm-trace future feature for query auditing |
| `chat-retry-from-user-message` | planned | — | circular-arrow icon-button on each user message; click rolls session back to immediately before that message and starts a fresh turn with the same text. Disabled while a turn is in flight. Available on both `core::agent` and `core::acp` paths |
| `chat-retry-truncates-session-history` | planned | — | retry trims every turn at-or-after the retried user message from `SessionState`; the session markdown file (under `chats/` per `chat-session-markdown-store`) is rewritten to match (markdown is truth); one `core::changes` row appended for the truncation. No hidden-branch archive in v1 |
| `chat-retry-resubmits-verbatim` | planned | — | retried turn carries the exact original user message text including `@`-mentions; context blocks (active note, `@<note-path>`, `@selection`) re-resolve at submit time. Message is not prefilled into the input — edit-and-resend is a separate future affordance |
| `chat-retry-command` | planned | — | new `chat_retry(session_id, user_message_id) -> Result<TurnId>` on the agent command surface. Implementation: truncate state + markdown → emit `TurnStarted` for the new id → invoke the same turn-driver as `chat_send`. No confirmation modal — the UI rollback animation conveys destructiveness |
| `chat-retry-acp` | planned | — | ACP path maps retry onto ending the current `session/prompt` line and starting a new one with the truncated history; falls back to spawning a fresh ACP session seeded with the pre-retry turns when the configured agent doesn't model history truncation natively |
| `chat-edit-and-resend` | planned | — | deferred — pencil-icon affordance on a user message drops its text back into the input for editing; same destructive trim as retry, but the resend is manual |
| `chat-retry-branches-session` | planned | — | deferred — keep the discarded tail as a named branch of the session so the user can compare outcomes |
| `chat-regenerate-assistant-message` | planned | — | deferred — regenerate-this-response button on an assistant bubble; same `chat_retry` plumbing anchored on the prior user turn |
| `chat-panel-default-height` | done | `core::config::VaultConfig::chat_height` (default `0.30`); `core::config::ELIGIBLE_VAULT` accepts the new `vault.chat_height` key with `ValueType::UnitFraction` validation (rejects values outside `[0.0, 1.0]`); `set_setting` persists via `toml_edit` so user comments survive. `app/src/chat/render.rs` reads `vault.chat_height` after settings load and applies the persisted fraction on vault open. |
| `chat-panel-expand-to-editor` | partial | `app/src/chat/render.rs` (expand button → open agent tab), `app/src/panels/agent.rs` (`agent`-kind tab body), `app/src/workbench_host.rs` (agent branch + agent-key autosave restore) | basic wiring landed: expand button → opens an `agent`-kind tab; the bottom chat region + resize handle hide while it's open. **Partial**: full expand-to-editor experience (session body rendered in tab, chat-region collapse/restore on agent-tab open/close) is still planned; current wiring is the foundations. |
| `chat-session-import-storage-path` | planned | imports land at `vault/<chats_dir>/imported/<source>-<YYYY-MM-DD>-<id>.md` — the visible `imported/` subfolder of `chats/`; same on-disk format as native sessions per `chat-session-markdown-store`; ordinary indexed notes, no `.hiker/` carve-out. Storage layer ready: `core::sessions::list` / `app::chat::session::discover` recurse into `chats/imported/`, and the sessions migration relocates legacy `.hiker/sessions/imported/` |
| `chat-session-import-converters` | planned | `core::sessions::import::<source>` per format; v1 candidates: Claude Code transcript JSON, Codex export, ChatGPT data-export JSON, Goose session log; preserves tool-call structure into hiker's `hiker-tool-call` block when the source carries it, otherwise lossy-text |
| `chat-session-import-sidecar` | planned | entry points: `hiker session import <path>` CLI + settings-UI affordance; sniff → convert → write → index; idempotent on `(source, source_id)` so re-imports update in place |
| `chat-session-imported-visibility` | planned | imported sessions are ordinary visible notes at `chats/imported/`, indexed + search-reachable; Vault mode groups them as a distinct "Imported sessions" provenance bucket (`vault-view-source-groups`) — no hide toggle |
| `chat-session-respawn-from-import` | planned | "Continue as chat" affordance on imported sessions creates a new native session at `vault/<chats_dir>/<date>-<new-id>.md` seeded with the imported turns (text-only by the lossy resume convention); basic chat agent picks up using current `[llm]` provider; new session's frontmatter records `respawned_from: imported/<source>-<date>-<id>.md`; ACP path seeds via Embedded Resource ContentBlock |
| `chat-session-import-live-sync` | planned | deferred — hooking into another agent's live session directory rather than ingesting exports |
| `chat-panel-agent-tab-edge-shadow` | planned | — | visual polish: add a top-edge drop shadow to the agent session window (chat panel / agent-tab body) so it reads as floating above the search-results / related-notes sections in the discovery panel. Today's sharp top border looks messy where it meets the sections region. Pairs with `chat-panel-pinned-bottom` (the chat region the shadow applies to) and `chat-panel-expand-to-editor` (agent-tab body needs the same treatment). CSS-only; no behavior change |
| `chat-new-session-reveals-panel` | done | `app/src/actions.rs::new_chat_session` calls `crate::chat::session::create_new`, then `ensure_panel_visible(PANEL_CHAT)` to insert the chat panel into the dock if it was removed, and sets `state.workbench.secondary_side_bar.visible = true` because the chat surface lives in the workbench's secondary side bar in the egui layout — re-inserting alone doesn't un-collapse a hidden side bar |


## Task queue (task-queue.md)

Unified work queue for non-interactive LLM work — note mutations, RAPTOR / fan-out, background single-shots. Producers submit; consumers are the in-process direct-LLM worker and any rmcp client (external HTTP, or the basic chat agent in-process when `expose_to_chat_agent` is on). Lands as a sibling to `core::llm` and `core::agent`. Spec is `task-queue.md`.

| Slug | Status | Evidence | Notes |
| ---- | ------ | -------- | ----- |
| `task-queue-core-module` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs` (`Queue`, `Slot`, broadcast event channel, lease table) | new `core::tasks` module (queue + lease table + event emitter) wired into `VaultSession` at the host |
| `task-queue-scope-non-chat` | partial | `core/src/tasks.rs` (`TaskKind` enum); `submit_note_mutation` producer in the host | note-mutation menu (the first non-chat producer) now flows through the queue; RAPTOR / auto-tag / summary-on-save producers are still planned, so the slug stays partial until they land |
| `task-queue-task-shape` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs` (`Task`, `TaskKind`, `TaskShape`, `Priority`, `TaskPayload`) | exhaustive `TaskKind` enum + `TaskShape::{Direct, Agent}` + `Priority::{Low, Normal, High}` |
| `task-queue-shape-routing` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::pick_next` (skips `Agent` shape for direct lane), `Queue::checkout_mcp` (no shape filter beyond opt-in) | direct worker only takes `Direct`; MCP clients can take either shape |
| `task-queue-io-worker-lane` | planned | a dedicated in-process worker for long-running non-LLM I/O (web crawl, `crawl-task-queue-lane`); distinct from the direct-LLM drain and the synchronous `NonLlmHandlers` path; never an MCP client; no `output_schema`; on the queue for lease/progress/cancel/visibility; per-page tasks roll up via `task-queue-task-grouping` |
| `task-queue-priority-tiers` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::Priority::rank`, `pick_next` ordering | strict High/Normal/Low ordering with FIFO inside via `submitted_at` |
| `task-queue-raptor-summarize` | partial | Enum variant + payload (Sprint A) plus a producer side (Sprint D): `cluster_regenerate_names` submits one task per non-user-edited cluster node at `Priority::Normal`. **Gap:** no concrete LLM-backed worker that consumes the task yet — the direct-LLM drain receives it but the worker that resolves members + writes the summary back through `Trees::set_summary` + `reset_churn` is the next chunk. Retry rides producer-side error handling |
| `task-queue-raptor-triage-match` | done | Enum variant + payload (Sprint A), producer side (Sprint D Phase 1) — `cluster_triage_enqueue` submits one `RaptorTriageMatch` task per saved-as-triage tree at `Priority::Normal` — and consumer side (Sprint D fix-up): the direct worker checks a `NonLlmHandlers` side-channel before falling back to the LLM (`core/src/tasks.rs::NonLlmHandlers::try_handle` + `drive_one_task`). `the host's `DirectWorkerHandlers`` implements the trait and dispatches `RaptorTriageMatch` to `core::suggest::triage_match` with the session-scoped trees / vault / staging / store handles and the live `[suggestions.triage]` config — same classifier the synchronous on-save fast path uses. Payload schema carries `tree_id` + `source_path` on the variant itself (no prompt body required). Author class is hardcoded `User` for now — see `triage-author-class` (partial) for the agent-author lift. Cancel-by-source-path remains a producer-side concern |
| `raptor-summarize-sample-merge` | partial | `core/src/cluster.rs::plan_sample_merge` + `SampleMergePlan { Single, SampleAndMerge { batches }, TooLarge { member_count } }` | pure planner: members partitioned into 30-per-batch siblings above the threshold, with a 300-member cap that routes to `TooLarge` (producer skips summarization for that cluster — no fallback path). Constants `SAMPLE_MERGE_BATCH_THRESHOLD` / `BATCH_SIZE` / `MEMBER_CAP` exported for producer reuse. **Gap:** no fan-in machinery in `core::tasks` yet — sibling batches submit independently; the merge step is the producer's responsibility (matches `task-queue-task-grouping` being deferred). When the producer lands, each batch is a `RaptorSummarize` task; the merge is a separate `RaptorSummarize` whose payload aggregates the partial summaries |
| `task-queue-lifecycle` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::TaskState`, `tick_maintenance` (lease-expiry requeue) | `Queued → Leased → Completed/Failed/Cancelled` with expiry-requeue path |
| `task-queue-terminal-retention` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::tick_maintenance` (GC past `terminal_retention_secs`); ticked every 2s from the host | terminal rows kept for `terminal_retention_secs` (default 60) then GC'd |
| `task-queue-lease-timeout` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::checkout_mcp` (clamps to `[1, max_secs]`), `heartbeat` extends, `tick_maintenance` requeues | external checkout stamps a lease + heartbeat extends; expiry requeues |
| `task-queue-submit-handle` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::TaskHandle::await_outcome` | producer gets a handle awaitable for `TaskOutcome`; drop is *not* implicit cancel (deviates from spec — see notes) |
| `task-queue-event-stream` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::QueueEvent`; emitted onto queue events from the host (broadcast→host bridge) | full event lifecycle on a single queue events channel |
| `task-queue-cancel-app-only` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::Queue::cancel` + `tasks_cancel` command (the host) | in-process function only; not exposed as an MCP tool |
| `task-queue-cancel-propagation-internal` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::Queue::cancel` fires the leased `StopSignal`; the direct worker observes via `tokio::select!` in `call_with_cancel` | mirrors `agent-chat-command-surface` cancel shape |
| `task-queue-stale-lease-rejection` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::Queue::cancel` flips state to `Cancelled` while resolving the producer handle; subsequent `submit_result`/`fail` return `QueueError::StaleLease`; MCP layer maps that to error code `1006` | producer handle resolves immediately; client learns at submit time |
| `task-queue-cancel-not-via-mcp` | done | `mcp-server/src/handler.rs` — no `task_cancel` tool advertised | cancel intentionally not in the MCP surface |
| `task-queue-direct-worker` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::run_direct_worker`; spawned per-parallelism in the host | tokio task drains `Direct`-shape tasks via `core::llm::chat` |
| `task-queue-direct-worker-toggle` | done | `core/src/config.rs::DirectWorkerConfig::enabled`; gate at the host (only spawns when enabled + `[llm] enabled`) | `[tasks] direct_worker.enabled` (default true) |
| `task-queue-structured-output` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::validate_against_schema` (subset JSON Schema validator); MCP `task_submit_inner` validates before completing | optional `output_schema`; lightweight schema validator (top-level `type` + `required` + nested `properties`) — full Draft-2020 deferred until a producer needs it |
| `task-queue-structured-output-direct` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::drive_one_task` (parse-and-retry-once with parse error appended; second failure → `schema_violation`) | provider-side enforcement deferred (graniet/`llm` doesn't expose a unified API); v1 fallback path is the spec's "ask for JSON, validate, retry once" |
| `task-queue-external-mcp-client` | done | `mcp-server/src/handler.rs::task_checkout_inner` stamps lease against `CLIENT_ID` with `McpClientVia::External` | rmcp HTTP clients pull tasks via `task_*`; identified by client id |
| `task-queue-expose-to-chat-agent` | done | `core/src/config.rs::TasksConfig::expose_to_chat_agent`; `mcp-server/src/handler.rs::dispatch_tool` gates `task_*` on `expose_tasks_to_chat_agent`; `mcp-server/src/agent_bridge.rs::agent_tool_defs_with(true)` advertises them; the host's chat module passes the flag through | toggle wired end-to-end through tool registry |
| `task-queue-mcp-client-handles-both-shapes` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::Queue::checkout_mcp` accepts shape filter but doesn't restrict by lane | MCP clients can take both `Direct` and `Agent` shapes |
| `task-queue-acp-via-mcp-only` | done | no ACP→queue wiring in `core::acp` (which doesn't exist yet); the queue only sees rmcp clients | enforced by absence — the queue is reachable only via `Queue::*` (in-process) and `task_*` MCP tools |
| `task-queue-worker-toggles` | done | `core/src/config.rs::TasksConfig` (independent `direct_worker.enabled` + `expose_to_chat_agent`); same toggles surfaced in `app/src/panels/settings/mod.rs`'s Task queue section | independent toggles |
| `task-queue-worker-preference` | done | `core/src/config.rs::WorkerPreferenceCfg`; `core/src/tasks.rs::Queue::submit` uses `direct_grace()` to set `eligible_to_direct_at` | `worker_preference = 'auto' | 'internal' | 'external'` arbitrates direct vs MCP |
| `task-queue-worker-preference-internal` | done | `core/src/config.rs::TasksConfig::direct_grace` returns 0s for `Internal`; `Queue::submit` sets eligibility to `now` | direct worker grabs immediately |
| `task-queue-worker-preference-external` | done | `direct_grace` returns 5s for `External` | direct worker waits 5s before becoming eligible |

| `task-queue-in-memory-only` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::QueueState` is a plain `HashMap` — no persistence | no on-disk persistence in v1; queue resets on vault swap |

| `task-queue-settings-ui-section` | done | `app/src/panels/settings/mod.rs` Task queue section + inline toggles in `app/src/panels/queue.rs` (direct-worker, expose-to-chat-agent, worker-preference) bound to `set_setting` with a status flash on save | both surfaces wired |
| `task-queue-respects-llm-disable` | done | the host skips spawning the direct worker when `!config.llm.enabled`; `mcp-server/src/handler.rs::guard_tasks` answers `1004 disabled` from every `task_*` tool when `llm_enabled = false`; home tile hidden in `app/src/panels/home.rs` | tools still surface in the rmcp registry but error coherently — agents see a uniform disabled state |
| `task-queue-home-widget` | partial | `app/src/panels/home.rs` (tasks section; subscribes to queue events, seeds via `tasks_snapshot`) | shipped as a section under the home overview rather than a fifth stat tile; active-count summary live-updates from queue events |
| `task-queue-home-detail-view` | done | `app/src/panels/queue.rs` | queue opens as a `queue`-kind tab per `tab-kinds`. Migrated from swap-out sub-mode to app-page tab (S2). |
| `task-queue-row-pulsing-leased` | done | `app/src/panels/queue.rs` (state pill + pulsing dot) | pulsing dot on Leased rows, worker label rendered |
| `task-queue-row-cancel-action` | done | `app/src/panels/queue.rs` (✕ button calls `tasks_cancel`); terminal rows omit the button | exactly the spec'd shape |
| `task-queue-home-widget-respects-llm-disable` | done | `app/src/panels/home.rs` reads `[llm] enabled` from settings at mount and hides the tasks section when disabled |
| `task-queue-audit-row` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::run_direct_worker` writes `surface = "core::tasks"`, `feature = task.kind.variant_name()`, with `task_id` / `worker` / `priority` / `duration_ms` in details | one row per terminal task from the direct worker; MCP-client-driven completions don't yet write the queue-side audit row (they write the `mcp-tool-call` row from `mcp-server/src/audit.rs` through the existing path) |
